+// Generate pseudo-random values for matrix initialization.
+constexpr double getPseudoRndValue(const size_t N, const size_t i,
+                                   const size_t j) noexcept {
+    return (((i + 1) * (i + j + 1) * 1299709) % (N * N)) /
+           static_cast<double>(N * N);
+}
+
+struct Block {
+    size_t begin;
+    size_t size;
+};
+
+// Divide n elements as evenly as possible, including when parts > n.
+Block getBlock(const size_t n, const int coordinate, const int parts) {
+    const size_t quotient = n / static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t remainder = n % static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t coord = static_cast<size_t>(coordinate);
+    return {coord * quotient + std::min(coord, remainder),
+            quotient + (coord < remainder ? 1U : 0U)};
 }
 
-void initMatrix(std::vector<double>& mat, const size_t N) {

-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            mat[i * N + j] = getPseudoRndValue(N, i, j);
+// Each process owns the A rows and B columns needed for its C tile.  The
+// deterministic initializer lets every process construct those pieces in
+// place, avoiding distribution collectives and a root-memory bottleneck.
+void initLocalMatrices(std::vector<double>& localA,
+                       std::vector<double>& localB, const size_t N,
+                       const Block rows, const Block columns) {
+    for (size_t localI = 0; localI < rows.size; ++localI) {
+        const size_t globalI = rows.begin + localI;
+        for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+            localA[localI * N + k] = getPseudoRndValue(N, globalI, k);
+        }
+    }
+
+    // B is stored as N x localColumns.  Thus B and C are contiguous in the
+    // inner j loop of the multiplication kernel.
+    for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+        for (size_t localJ = 0; localJ < columns.size; ++localJ) {
+            const size_t globalJ = columns.begin + localJ;
+            localB[k * columns.size + localJ] =
+                getPseudoRndValue(N, k, globalJ);
         }
     }
 }
 
-void matrixMultiply(const std::vector<double>& A, const std::vector<double>& B, 
-                    std::vector<double>& C, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            double sum = 0.0;
-            for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
-                sum += A[i * N + k] * B[k * N + j];
+// Cache-blocked matrix multiplication for the process-local C tile.  k is
+// ordered exactly as in the scalar implementation for every C element, while
+// j is independent and can be vectorized without a floating-point reduction.
+void matrixMultiply(const double* __restrict__ localA,
+                    const double* __restrict__ localB,
+                    double* __restrict__ localC, const size_t N,
+                    const size_t localRows, const size_t localColumns) {
+    constexpr size_t rowTile = 32;
+    constexpr size_t columnTile = 128;
+    constexpr size_t innerTile = 128;
+
+    for (size_t ii = 0; ii < localRows; ii += rowTile) {
+        const size_t iEnd = std::min(ii + rowTile, localRows);
+        for (size_t jj = 0; jj < localColumns; jj += columnTile) {
+            const size_t jEnd = std::min(jj + columnTile, localColumns);
+            for (size_t kk = 0; kk < N; kk += innerTile) {
+                const size_t kEnd = std::min(kk + innerTile, N);
+                for (size_t k = kk; k < kEnd; ++k) {
+                    const double* __restrict__ bRow =
+                        localB + k * localColumns;
+                    for (size_t i = ii; i < iEnd; ++i) {
+                        const double a = localA[i * N + k];
+                        double* __restrict__ cRow =
+                            localC + i * localColumns;
+#pragma omp simd
+                        for (size_t j = jj; j < jEnd; ++j) {
+                            cRow[j] += a * bRow[j];
+                        }
+                    }
+                }
             }
-            C[i * N + j] = sum;
         }
     }
 }

+// MPI uses int counts in the portable interfaces available on MPI-3 systems.
+// Chunking also lets the benchmark handle rows wider than INT_MAX elements.
+void sendLarge(const double* data, size_t count, const int destination,
+               const int tag, MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Send(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, destination, tag, communicator);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+void receiveLarge(double* data, size_t count, const int source, const int tag,
+                  MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Recv(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, source, tag, communicator,
+                 MPI_STATUS_IGNORE);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+// Assemble C only when the user requests result output.  Rows are received
+// directly into their final locations, so rank zero needs no packed staging
+// buffer and MPI int-count limits do not constrain the matrix size.
+std::vector<double> gatherResult(const std::vector<double>& localC,
+                                 const size_t N, const Block localRows,
+                                 const Block localColumns, const int rank,
+                                 const int worldSize, const int gridRows,
+                                 const int gridColumns,
+                                 MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr int resultTag = 1701;
+
+    if (rank != 0) {
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < localRows.size; ++i) {
+            sendLarge(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                      localColumns.size, 0, resultTag, communicator);
+        }
+        return {};
+    }
+
+    std::vector<double> result(N * N);
+    for (int source = 0; source < worldSize; ++source) {
+        const int rowCoordinate = source / gridColumns;
+        const int columnCoordinate = source % gridColumns;
+        const Block rows = getBlock(N, rowCoordinate, gridRows);
+        const Block columns = getBlock(N, columnCoordinate, gridColumns);
+
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < rows.size; ++i) {
+            double* destination =
+                result.data() + (rows.begin + i) * N + columns.begin;
+            if (source == 0) {
+                std::copy_n(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                            columns.size, destination);
+            } else {
+                receiveLarge(destination, columns.size, source, resultTag,
+                             communicator);
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    return result;
+}

+// Generate pseudo-random values for matrix initialization.
+constexpr double getPseudoRndValue(const size_t N, const size_t i,
+                                   const size_t j) noexcept {
+    return (((i + 1) * (i + j + 1) * 1299709) % (N * N)) /
+           static_cast<double>(N * N);
+}
+
+struct Block {
+    size_t begin;
+    size_t size;
+};
+
+// Divide n elements as evenly as possible, including when parts > n.
+Block getBlock(const size_t n, const int coordinate, const int parts) {
+    const size_t quotient = n / static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t remainder = n % static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t coord = static_cast<size_t>(coordinate);
+    return {coord * quotient + std::min(coord, remainder),
+            quotient + (coord < remainder ? 1U : 0U)};
 }
 
-void initMatrix(std::vector<double>& mat, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            mat[i * N + j] = getPseudoRndValue(N, i, j);
+// Each process owns the A rows and B columns needed for its C tile.  The
+// deterministic initializer lets every process construct those pieces in
+// place, avoiding distribution collectives and a root-memory bottleneck.
+void initLocalMatrices(std::vector<double>& localA,
+                       std::vector<double>& localB, const size_t N,
+                       const Block rows, const Block columns) {
+    for (size_t localI = 0; localI < rows.size; ++localI) {
+        const size_t globalI = rows.begin + localI;
+        for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+            localA[localI * N + k] = getPseudoRndValue(N, globalI, k);
+        }
+    }
+
+    // B is stored as N x localColumns.  Thus B and C are contiguous in the
+    // inner j loop of the multiplication kernel.
+    for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+        for (size_t localJ = 0; localJ < columns.size; ++localJ) {
+            const size_t globalJ = columns.begin + localJ;
+            localB[k * columns.size + localJ] =
+                getPseudoRndValue(N, k, globalJ);
         }
     }
 }
 
-void matrixMultiply(const std::vector<double>& A, const std::vector<double>& B, 
-                    std::vector<double>& C, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            double sum = 0.0;
-            for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
-                sum += A[i * N + k] * B[k * N + j];
+// Cache-blocked matrix multiplication for the process-local C tile.  k is
+// ordered exactly as in the scalar implementation for every C element, while
+// j is independent and can be vectorized without a floating-point reduction.
+void matrixMultiply(const double* __restrict__ localA,
+                    const double* __restrict__ localB,
+                    double* __restrict__ localC, const size_t N,
+                    const size_t localRows, const size_t localColumns) {
+    constexpr size_t rowTile = 32;
+    constexpr size_t columnTile = 128;
+    constexpr size_t innerTile = 128;
+
+    for (size_t ii = 0; ii < localRows; ii += rowTile) {
+        const size_t iEnd = std::min(ii + rowTile, localRows);
+        for (size_t jj = 0; jj < localColumns; jj += columnTile) {
+            const size_t jEnd = std::min(jj + columnTile, localColumns);
+            for (size_t kk = 0; kk < N; kk += innerTile) {
+                const size_t kEnd = std::min(kk + innerTile, N);
+                for (size_t k = kk; k < kEnd; ++k) {
+                    const double* __restrict__ bRow =
+                        localB + k * localColumns;
+                    for (size_t i = ii; i < iEnd; ++i) {
+                        const double a = localA[i * N + k];
+                        double* __restrict__ cRow =
+                            localC + i * localColumns;
+#pragma omp simd
+                        for (size_t j = jj; j < jEnd; ++j) {
+                            cRow[j] += a * bRow[j];
+                        }
+                    }
+                }
             }
-            C[i * N + j] = sum;
         }
     }
 }

+// MPI uses int counts in the portable interfaces available on MPI-3 systems.
+// Chunking also lets the benchmark handle rows wider than INT_MAX elements.
+void sendLarge(const double* data, size_t count, const int destination,
+               const int tag, MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Send(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, destination, tag, communicator);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+void receiveLarge(double* data, size_t count, const int source, const int tag,
+                  MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Recv(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, source, tag, communicator,
+                 MPI_STATUS_IGNORE);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+// Assemble C only when the user requests result output.  Rows are received
+// directly into their final locations, so rank zero needs no packed staging
+// buffer and MPI int-count limits do not constrain the matrix size.
+std::vector<double> gatherResult(const std::vector<double>& localC,
+                                 const size_t N, const Block localRows,
+                                 const Block localColumns, const int rank,
+                                 const int worldSize, const int gridRows,
+                                 const int gridColumns,
+                                 MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr int resultTag = 1701;
+
+    if (rank != 0) {
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < localRows.size; ++i) {
+            sendLarge(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                      localColumns.size, 0, resultTag, communicator);
+        }
+        return {};
+    }
+
+    std::vector<double> result(N * N);
+    for (int source = 0; source < worldSize; ++source) {
+        const int rowCoordinate = source / gridColumns;
+        const int columnCoordinate = source % gridColumns;
+        const Block rows = getBlock(N, rowCoordinate, gridRows);
+        const Block columns = getBlock(N, columnCoordinate, gridColumns);
+
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < rows.size; ++i) {
+            double* destination =
+                result.data() + (rows.begin + i) * N + columns.begin;
+            if (source == 0) {
+                std::copy_n(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                            columns.size, destination);
+            } else {
+                receiveLarge(destination, columns.size, source, resultTag,
+                             communicator);
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    return result;
+}

+// Generate pseudo-random values for matrix initialization.
+constexpr double getPseudoRndValue(const size_t N, const size_t i,
+                                   const size_t j) noexcept {
+    return (((i + 1) * (i + j + 1) * 1299709) % (N * N)) /
+           static_cast<double>(N * N);
+}
+
+struct Block {
+    size_t begin;
+    size_t size;
+};
+
+// Divide n elements as evenly as possible, including when parts > n.
+Block getBlock(const size_t n, const int coordinate, const int parts) {
+    const size_t quotient = n / static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t remainder = n % static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t coord = static_cast<size_t>(coordinate);
+    return {coord * quotient + std::min(coord, remainder),
+            quotient + (coord < remainder ? 1U : 0U)};
 }
 
-void initMatrix(std::vector<double>& mat, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            mat[i * N + j] = getPseudoRndValue(N, i, j);
+// Each process owns the A rows and B columns needed for its C tile.  The
+// deterministic initializer lets every process construct those pieces in
+// place, avoiding distribution collectives and a root-memory bottleneck.
+void initLocalMatrices(std::vector<double>& localA,
+                       std::vector<double>& localB, const size_t N,
+                       const Block rows, const Block columns) {
+    for (size_t localI = 0; localI < rows.size; ++localI) {
+        const size_t globalI = rows.begin + localI;
+        for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+            localA[localI * N + k] = getPseudoRndValue(N, globalI, k);
+        }
+    }
+
+    // B is stored as N x localColumns.  Thus B and C are contiguous in the
+    // inner j loop of the multiplication kernel.
+    for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+        for (size_t localJ = 0; localJ < columns.size; ++localJ) {
+            const size_t globalJ = columns.begin + localJ;
+            localB[k * columns.size + localJ] =
+                getPseudoRndValue(N, k, globalJ);
         }
     }
 }
 
-void matrixMultiply(const std::vector<double>& A, const std::vector<double>& B, 
-                    std::vector<double>& C, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            double sum = 0.0;
-            for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
-                sum += A[i * N + k] * B[k * N + j];
+// Cache-blocked matrix multiplication for the process-local C tile.  k is
+// ordered exactly as in the scalar implementation for every C element, while
+// j is independent and can be vectorized without a floating-point reduction.
+void matrixMultiply(const double* __restrict__ localA,
+                    const double* __restrict__ localB,
+                    double* __restrict__ localC, const size_t N,
+                    const size_t localRows, const size_t localColumns) {
+    constexpr size_t rowTile = 32;
+    constexpr size_t columnTile = 128;
+    constexpr size_t innerTile = 128;
+
+    for (size_t ii = 0; ii < localRows; ii += rowTile) {
+        const size_t iEnd = std::min(ii + rowTile, localRows);
+        for (size_t jj = 0; jj < localColumns; jj += columnTile) {
+            const size_t jEnd = std::min(jj + columnTile, localColumns);
+            for (size_t kk = 0; kk < N; kk += innerTile) {
+                const size_t kEnd = std::min(kk + innerTile, N);
+                for (size_t k = kk; k < kEnd; ++k) {
+                    const double* __restrict__ bRow =
+                        localB + k * localColumns;
+                    for (size_t i = ii; i < iEnd; ++i) {
+                        const double a = localA[i * N + k];
+                        double* __restrict__ cRow =
+                            localC + i * localColumns;
+#pragma omp simd
+                        for (size_t j = jj; j < jEnd; ++j) {
+                            cRow[j] += a * bRow[j];
+                        }
+                    }
+                }
             }
-            C[i * N + j] = sum;
         }
     }
 }

+// MPI uses int counts in the portable interfaces available on MPI-3 systems.
+// Chunking also lets the benchmark handle rows wider than INT_MAX elements.
+void sendLarge(const double* data, size_t count, const int destination,
+               const int tag, MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Send(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, destination, tag, communicator);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+void receiveLarge(double* data, size_t count, const int source, const int tag,
+                  MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Recv(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, source, tag, communicator,
+                 MPI_STATUS_IGNORE);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+// Assemble C only when the user requests result output.  Rows are received
+// directly into their final locations, so rank zero needs no packed staging
+// buffer and MPI int-count limits do not constrain the matrix size.
+std::vector<double> gatherResult(const std::vector<double>& localC,
+                                 const size_t N, const Block localRows,
+                                 const Block localColumns, const int rank,
+                                 const int worldSize, const int gridRows,
+                                 const int gridColumns,
+                                 MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr int resultTag = 1701;
+
+    if (rank != 0) {
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < localRows.size; ++i) {
+            sendLarge(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                      localColumns.size, 0, resultTag, communicator);
+        }
+        return {};
+    }
+
+    std::vector<double> result(N * N);
+    for (int source = 0; source < worldSize; ++source) {
+        const int rowCoordinate = source / gridColumns;
+        const int columnCoordinate = source % gridColumns;
+        const Block rows = getBlock(N, rowCoordinate, gridRows);
+        const Block columns = getBlock(N, columnCoordinate, gridColumns);
+
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < rows.size; ++i) {
+            double* destination =
+                result.data() + (rows.begin + i) * N + columns.begin;
+            if (source == 0) {
+                std::copy_n(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                            columns.size, destination);
+            } else {
+                receiveLarge(destination, columns.size, source, resultTag,
+                             communicator);
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    return result;
+}

+    std::printf("  -v           Enable validation\n");

 }

+    const size_t coord = static_cast<size_t>(coordinate);
+    return {coord * quotient + std::min(coord, remainder),
+            quotient + (coord < remainder ? 1U : 0U)};
 }

+// Each process owns the A rows and B columns needed for its C tile.  The
+// deterministic initializer lets every process construct those pieces in

+void initLocalMatrices(std::vector<double>& localA,

+                       const Block rows, const Block columns) {

-void matrixMultiply(const std::vector<double>& A, const std::vector<double>& B, 
-                    std::vector<double>& C, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            double sum = 0.0;
-            for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
-                sum += A[i * N + k] * B[k * N + j];
+// Cache-blocked matrix multiplication for the process-local C tile.  k is
+// ordered exactly as in the scalar implementation for every C element, while
+// j is independent and can be vectorized without a floating-point reduction.
+void matrixMultiply(const double* __restrict__ localA,
+                    const double* __restrict__ localB,
+                    double* __restrict__ localC, const size_t N,
+                    const size_t localRows, const size_t localColumns) {
+    constexpr size_t rowTile = 32;
+    constexpr size_t columnTile = 128;
+    constexpr size_t innerTile = 128;
+
+    for (size_t ii = 0; ii < localRows; ii += rowTile) {
+        const size_t iEnd = std::min(ii + rowTile, localRows);
+        for (size_t jj = 0; jj < localColumns; jj += columnTile) {
+            const size_t jEnd = std::min(jj + columnTile, localColumns);
+            for (size_t kk = 0; kk < N; kk += innerTile) {
+                const size_t kEnd = std::min(kk + innerTile, N);
+                for (size_t k = kk; k < kEnd; ++k) {
+                    const double* __restrict__ bRow =
+                        localB + k * localColumns;
+                    for (size_t i = ii; i < iEnd; ++i) {
+                        const double a = localA[i * N + k];
+                        double* __restrict__ cRow =
+                            localC + i * localColumns;
+#pragma omp simd
+                        for (size_t j = jj; j < jEnd; ++j) {
+                            cRow[j] += a * bRow[j];
+                        }
+                    }
+                }
             }
-            C[i * N + j] = sum;
         }
     }
 }

+// MPI uses int counts in the portable interfaces available on MPI-3 systems.
+// Chunking also lets the benchmark handle rows wider than INT_MAX elements.
+void sendLarge(const double* data, size_t count, const int destination,
+               const int tag, MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Send(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, destination, tag, communicator);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+void receiveLarge(double* data, size_t count, const int source, const int tag,
+                  MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Recv(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, source, tag, communicator,
+                 MPI_STATUS_IGNORE);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+// Assemble C only when the user requests result output.  Rows are received
+// directly into their final locations, so rank zero needs no packed staging
+// buffer and MPI int-count limits do not constrain the matrix size.
+std::vector<double> gatherResult(const std::vector<double>& localC,
+                                 const size_t N, const Block localRows,
+                                 const Block localColumns, const int rank,
+                                 const int worldSize, const int gridRows,
+                                 const int gridColumns,
+                                 MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr int resultTag = 1701;
+
+    if (rank != 0) {
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < localRows.size; ++i) {
+            sendLarge(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                      localColumns.size, 0, resultTag, communicator);
+        }
+        return {};
+    }
+
+    std::vector<double> result(N * N);
+    for (int source = 0; source < worldSize; ++source) {
+        const int rowCoordinate = source / gridColumns;
+        const int columnCoordinate = source % gridColumns;
+        const Block rows = getBlock(N, rowCoordinate, gridRows);
+        const Block columns = getBlock(N, columnCoordinate, gridColumns);
+
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < rows.size; ++i) {
+            double* destination =
+                result.data() + (rows.begin + i) * N + columns.begin;
+            if (source == 0) {
+                std::copy_n(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                            columns.size, destination);
+            } else {
+                receiveLarge(destination, columns.size, source, resultTag,
+                             communicator);
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    return result;
+}
+

-    printf("  -v           Enable validation\n");
-    printf("  -r           Print results for external validation\n");
-    printf("  -h           Show this help message\n");
+    std::printf("Usage: %s [options]\n", progName);
+    std::printf("Options:\n");
+    std::printf(
+        "  -n <num>     Matrix size N (computes NxN * NxN) (default: 512)\n");
+    std::printf("  -v           Enable validation\n");
+    std::printf("  -r           Print results for external validation\n");
+    std::printf("  -h           Show this help message\n");
 }

+// MPI uses int counts in the portable interfaces available on MPI-3 systems.
+// Chunking also lets the benchmark handle rows wider than INT_MAX elements.
+void sendLarge(const double* data, size_t count, const int destination,
+               const int tag, MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Send(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, destination, tag, communicator);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+void receiveLarge(double* data, size_t count, const int source, const int tag,
+                  MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Recv(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, source, tag, communicator,
+                 MPI_STATUS_IGNORE);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+// Assemble C only when the user requests result output.  Rows are received
+// directly into their final locations, so rank zero needs no packed staging
+// buffer and MPI int-count limits do not constrain the matrix size.
+std::vector<double> gatherResult(const std::vector<double>& localC,
+                                 const size_t N, const Block localRows,
+                                 const Block localColumns, const int rank,
+                                 const int worldSize, const int gridRows,
+                                 const int gridColumns,
+                                 MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr int resultTag = 1701;
+
+    if (rank != 0) {
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < localRows.size; ++i) {
+            sendLarge(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                      localColumns.size, 0, resultTag, communicator);
+        }
+        return {};
+    }
+
+    std::vector<double> result(N * N);
+    for (int source = 0; source < worldSize; ++source) {
+        const int rowCoordinate = source / gridColumns;
+        const int columnCoordinate = source % gridColumns;
+        const Block rows = getBlock(N, rowCoordinate, gridRows);
+        const Block columns = getBlock(N, columnCoordinate, gridColumns);
+
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < rows.size; ++i) {
+            double* destination =
+                result.data() + (rows.begin + i) * N + columns.begin;
+            if (source == 0) {
+                std::copy_n(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                            columns.size, destination);
+            } else {
+                receiveLarge(destination, columns.size, source, resultTag,
+                             communicator);
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    return result;
+}

+// Generate pseudo-random values for matrix initialization.
+constexpr double getPseudoRndValue(const size_t N, const size_t i,
+                                   const size_t j) noexcept {
+    return (((i + 1) * (i + j + 1) * 1299709) % (N * N)) /
+           static_cast<double>(N * N);
+}
+
+struct Block {
+    size_t begin;
+    size_t size;
+};
+
+// Divide n elements as evenly as possible, including when parts > n.
+Block getBlock(const size_t n, const int coordinate, const int parts) {
+    const size_t quotient = n / static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t remainder = n % static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t coord = static_cast<size_t>(coordinate);
+    return {coord * quotient + std::min(coord, remainder),
+            quotient + (coord < remainder ? 1U : 0U)};
 }
 
-void initMatrix(std::vector<double>& mat, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            mat[i * N + j] = getPseudoRndValue(N, i, j);
+// Each process owns the A rows and B columns needed for its C tile.  The
+// deterministic initializer lets every process construct those pieces in
+// place, avoiding distribution collectives and a root-memory bottleneck.
+void initLocalMatrices(std::vector<double>& localA,
+                       std::vector<double>& localB, const size_t N,
+                       const Block rows, const Block columns) {
+    for (size_t localI = 0; localI < rows.size; ++localI) {
+        const size_t globalI = rows.begin + localI;
+        for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+            localA[localI * N + k] = getPseudoRndValue(N, globalI, k);
+        }
+    }
+
+    // B is stored as N x localColumns.  Thus B and C are contiguous in the
+    // inner j loop of the multiplication kernel.
+    for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+        for (size_t localJ = 0; localJ < columns.size; ++localJ) {
+            const size_t globalJ = columns.begin + localJ;
+            localB[k * columns.size + localJ] =
+                getPseudoRndValue(N, k, globalJ);
         }
     }
 }
 
-void matrixMultiply(const std::vector<double>& A, const std::vector<double>& B, 
-                    std::vector<double>& C, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            double sum = 0.0;
-            for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
-                sum += A[i * N + k] * B[k * N + j];
+// Cache-blocked matrix multiplication for the process-local C tile.  k is
+// ordered exactly as in the scalar implementation for every C element, while
+// j is independent and can be vectorized without a floating-point reduction.
+void matrixMultiply(const double* __restrict__ localA,
+                    const double* __restrict__ localB,
+                    double* __restrict__ localC, const size_t N,
+                    const size_t localRows, const size_t localColumns) {
+    constexpr size_t rowTile = 32;
+    constexpr size_t columnTile = 128;
+    constexpr size_t innerTile = 128;
+
+    for (size_t ii = 0; ii < localRows; ii += rowTile) {
+        const size_t iEnd = std::min(ii + rowTile, localRows);
+        for (size_t jj = 0; jj < localColumns; jj += columnTile) {
+            const size_t jEnd = std::min(jj + columnTile, localColumns);
+            for (size_t kk = 0; kk < N; kk += innerTile) {
+                const size_t kEnd = std::min(kk + innerTile, N);
+                for (size_t k = kk; k < kEnd; ++k) {
+                    const double* __restrict__ bRow =
+                        localB + k * localColumns;
+                    for (size_t i = ii; i < iEnd; ++i) {
+                        const double a = localA[i * N + k];
+                        double* __restrict__ cRow =
+                            localC + i * localColumns;
+#pragma omp simd
+                        for (size_t j = jj; j < jEnd; ++j) {
+                            cRow[j] += a * bRow[j];
+                        }
+                    }
+                }
             }
-            C[i * N + j] = sum;
         }
     }
 }

    13	// Generate pseudo-random values for matrix initialization.
    14	constexpr double getPseudoRndValue(const size_t N, const size_t i,
    15	                                   const size_t j) noexcept {
    16	    return (((i + 1) * (i + j + 1) * 1299709) % (N * N)) /
    17	           static_cast<double>(N * N);
    18	}
    19	
    20	struct Block {
    21	    size_t begin;
    22	    size_t size;
    23	};
    24	
    25	// Divide n elements as evenly as possible, including when parts > n.
    26	Block getBlock(const size_t n, const int coordinate, const int parts) {
    27	    const size_t quotient = n / static_cast<size_t>(parts);
    28	    const size_t remainder = n % static_cast<size_t>(parts);
    29	    const size_t coord = static_cast<size_t>(coordinate);
    30	    return {coord * quotient + std::min(coord, remainder),
    31	            quotient + (coord < remainder ? 1U : 0U)};
    32	}
    33	
    34	// Each process owns the A rows and B columns needed for its C tile.  The
    35	// deterministic initializer lets every process construct those pieces in
    36	// place, avoiding distribution collectives and a root-memory bottleneck.
    37	void initLocalMatrices(std::vector<double>& localA,
    38	                       std::vector<double>& localB, const size_t N,
    39	                       const Block rows, const Block columns) {
    40	    for (size_t localI = 0; localI < rows.size; ++localI) {
    41	        const size_t globalI = rows.begin + localI;
    42	        for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
    43	            localA[localI * N + k] = getPseudoRndValue(N, globalI, k);
    44	        }
    45	    }
    46	
    47	    // B is stored as N x localColumns.  Thus B and C are contiguous in the
    48	    // inner j loop of the multiplication kernel.
    49	    for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
    50	        for (size_t localJ = 0; localJ < columns.size; ++localJ) {
    51	            const size_t globalJ = columns.begin + localJ;
    52	            localB[k * columns.size + localJ] =
    53	                getPseudoRndValue(N, k, globalJ);
    54	        }
    55	    }
    56	}
    57	
    58	// Cache-blocked matrix multiplication for the process-local C tile.  k is
    59	// ordered exactly as in the scalar implementation for every C element, while
    60	// j is independent and can be vectorized without a floating-point reduction.
    61	void matrixMultiply(const double* __restrict__ localA,
    62	                    const double* __restrict__ localB,
    63	                    double* __restrict__ localC, const size_t N,
    64	                    const size_t localRows, const size_t localColumns) {
    65	    constexpr size_t rowTile = 32;
    66	    constexpr size_t columnTile = 128;
    67	    constexpr size_t innerTile = 128;
    68	
    69	    for (size_t ii = 0; ii < localRows; ii += rowTile) {
    70	        const size_t iEnd = std::min(ii + rowTile, localRows);
    71	        for (size_t jj = 0; jj < localColumns; jj += columnTile) {
    72	            const size_t jEnd = std::min(jj + columnTile, localColumns);
    73	            for (size_t kk = 0; kk < N; kk += innerTile) {
    74	                const size_t kEnd = std::min(kk + innerTile, N);
    75	                for (size_t k = kk; k < kEnd; ++k) {
    76	                    const double* __restrict__ bRow =
    77	                        localB + k * localColumns;
    78	                    for (size_t i = ii; i < iEnd; ++i) {
    79	                        const double a = localA[i * N + k];
    80	                        double* __restrict__ cRow =
    81	                            localC + i * localColumns;
    82	#pragma omp simd
    83	                        for (size_t j = jj; j < jEnd; ++j) {
    84	                            cRow[j] += a * bRow[j];
    85	                        }
    86	                    }
    87	                }
    88	            }
    89	        }
    90	    }
    91	}
    92	
    93	bool validateLocalResult(const std::vector<double>& localA,
    94	                         const std::vector<double>& localB,
    95	                         const std::vector<double>& localC, const size_t N,
    96	                         const Block rows, const Block columns,
    97	                         const int rank) {
    98	    constexpr size_t checkPoints[] = {0, 1, 2, 3, 4};
    99	
   100	    for (const size_t rowPoint : checkPoints) {
   101	        const size_t globalI = rowPoint % N;
   102	        if (globalI < rows.begin || globalI >= rows.begin + rows.size) {
   103	            continue;
   104	        }
   105	        const size_t localI = globalI - rows.begin;
   106	
   107	        for (const size_t columnPoint : checkPoints) {
   108	            const size_t globalJ = columnPoint % N;
   109	            if (globalJ < columns.begin ||
   110	                globalJ >= columns.begin + columns.size) {
   111	                continue;
   112	            }
   113	            const size_t localJ = globalJ - columns.begin;
   114	
   115	            double expected = 0.0;
   116	            for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
   117	                expected += localA[localI * N + k] *
   118	                            localB[k * columns.size + localJ];
   119	            }
   120	
   121	            const double actual =
   122	                localC[localI * columns.size + localJ];
   123	            const double relError =
   124	                std::abs((actual - expected) / (expected + 1e-10));
   125	            if (relError > 1e-6) {
   126	                std::printf(
   127	                    "Validation failed on rank %d at (%zu, %zu): expected "
   128	                    "%.10f, got %.10f (error: %.10e)\n",
   129	                    rank, globalI, globalJ, expected, actual, relError);
   130	                return false;
   131	            }
   132	        }
   133	    }
   134	
   135	    return true;
   136	}
   137	
   138	// MPI uses int counts in the portable interfaces available on MPI-3 systems.
   139	// Chunking also lets the benchmark handle rows wider than INT_MAX elements.
   140	void sendLarge(const double* data, size_t count, const int destination,
   141	               const int tag, MPI_Comm communicator) {
   142	    constexpr size_t maxCount =
   143	        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
   144	    while (count != 0) {
   145	        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
   146	        MPI_Send(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, destination, tag, communicator);
   147	        data += chunk;
   148	        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
   149	    }
   150	}
   151	
   152	void receiveLarge(double* data, size_t count, const int source, const int tag,
   153	                  MPI_Comm communicator) {
   154	    constexpr size_t maxCount =
   155	        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
   156	    while (count != 0) {
   157	        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
   158	        MPI_Recv(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, source, tag, communicator,
   159	                 MPI_STATUS_IGNORE);
   160	        data += chunk;
   161	        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
   162	    }
   163	}
   164	
   165	// Assemble C only when the user requests result output.  Rows are received
   166	// directly into their final locations, so rank zero needs no packed staging
   167	// buffer and MPI int-count limits do not constrain the matrix size.
   168	std::vector<double> gatherResult(const std::vector<double>& localC,
   169	                                 const size_t N, const Block localRows,
   170	                                 const Block localColumns, const int rank,
   171	                                 const int worldSize, const int gridRows,
   172	                                 const int gridColumns,
   173	                                 MPI_Comm communicator) {
   174	    constexpr int resultTag = 1701;
   175	
   176	    if (rank != 0) {
   177	        for (size_t i = 0; i < localRows.size; ++i) {
   178	            sendLarge(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
   179	                      localColumns.size, 0, resultTag, communicator);
   180	        }
   181	        return {};
   182	    }
   183	
   184	    std::vector<double> result(N * N);
   185	    for (int source = 0; source < worldSize; ++source) {
   186	        const int rowCoordinate = source / gridColumns;
   187	        const int columnCoordinate = source % gridColumns;
   188	        const Block rows = getBlock(N, rowCoordinate, gridRows);
   189	        const Block columns = getBlock(N, columnCoordinate, gridColumns);
   190	
   191	        for (size_t i = 0; i < rows.size; ++i) {
   192	            double* destination =
   193	                result.data() + (rows.begin + i) * N + columns.begin;
   194	            if (source == 0) {
   195	                std::copy_n(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
   196	                            columns.size, destination);
   197	            } else {
   198	                receiveLarge(destination, columns.size, source, resultTag,
   199	                             communicator);
   200	            }
   201	        }
   202	    }
   203	
   204	    return result;
   205	}

   207	void printUsage(const char* progName) {
   208	    std::printf("Usage: %s [options]\n", progName);
   209	    std::printf("Options:\n");
   210	    std::printf(
   211	        "  -n <num>     Matrix size N (computes NxN * NxN) (default: 512)\n");
   212	    std::printf("  -v           Enable validation\n");
   213	    std::printf("  -r           Print results for external validation\n");
   214	    std::printf("  -h           Show this help message\n");
   215	}

   217	int main(int argc, char** argv) {
   218	    MPI_Init(&argc, &argv);
   219	
   220	    int rank = 0;
   221	    int worldSize = 1;
   222	    MPI_Comm_rank(MPI_COMM_WORLD, &rank);
   223	    MPI_Comm_size(MPI_COMM_WORLD, &worldSize);
   224	
   225	    size_t N = 512;
   226	    bool validate = false;
   227	    bool printResults = false;
   228	    bool showHelp = false;
   229	    bool argumentsValid = true;
   230	
   231	    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {
   232	        if (std::strcmp(argv[i], "-n") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {
   233	            const long long parsed = std::atoll(argv[++i]);
   234	            if (parsed <= 0) {
   235	                argumentsValid = false;
   236	            } else {
   237	                N = static_cast<size_t>(parsed);
   238	            }
   239	        } else if (std::strcmp(argv[i], "-v") == 0) {
   240	            validate = true;
   241	        } else if (std::strcmp(argv[i], "-r") == 0) {
   242	            printResults = true;
   243	        } else if (std::strcmp(argv[i], "-h") == 0) {
   244	            showHelp = true;
   245	        } else {
   246	            if (rank == 0) {
   247	                std::printf("Unknown option: %s\n", argv[i]);
   248	            }
   249	            argumentsValid = false;
   250	        }
   251	    }
   252	
   253	    if (showHelp || !argumentsValid) {
   254	        if (rank == 0) {
   255	            if (!argumentsValid) {
   256	                std::printf("Matrix size must be a positive integer.\n");
   257	            }
   258	            printUsage(argv[0]);
   259	        }
   260	        MPI_Finalize();
   261	        return argumentsValid ? 0 : 1;
   262	    }
   263	
   264	    int grid[2] = {0, 0};
   265	    MPI_Dims_create(worldSize, 2, grid);
   266	    const int rowCoordinate = rank / grid[1];
   267	    const int columnCoordinate = rank % grid[1];
   268	    const Block localRows = getBlock(N, rowCoordinate, grid[0]);
   269	    const Block localColumns = getBlock(N, columnCoordinate, grid[1]);
   270	
   271	    if (rank == 0) {
   272	        std::printf("Matrix Multiplication Benchmark\n");
   273	        std::printf("Matrix size: %zu x %zu\n", N, N);
   274	        std::printf("Validation: %s\n", validate ? "enabled" : "disabled");
   275	        std::printf("MPI processes: %d (%d x %d grid)\n", worldSize, grid[0],
   276	                    grid[1]);
   277	        std::printf("Initializing matrices...\n");
   278	    }
   279	
   280	    std::vector<double> localA(localRows.size * N);
   281	    std::vector<double> localB(N * localColumns.size);
   282	    std::vector<double> localC(localRows.size * localColumns.size, 0.0);
   283	    initLocalMatrices(localA, localB, N, localRows, localColumns);
   284	
   285	    if (rank == 0) {
   286	        std::printf("Computing matrix multiplication...\n");
   287	        std::fflush(stdout);
   288	    }
   289	    MPI_Barrier(MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   290	    const double start = MPI_Wtime();
   291	
   292	    matrixMultiply(localA.data(), localB.data(), localC.data(), N,
   293	                   localRows.size, localColumns.size);
   294	
   295	    const double localElapsed = MPI_Wtime() - start;
   296	    double elapsed = 0.0;
   297	    MPI_Reduce(&localElapsed, &elapsed, 1, MPI_DOUBLE, MPI_MAX, 0,
   298	               MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   299	
   300	    if (rank == 0) {
   301	        const long long durationMs = static_cast<long long>(elapsed * 1000.0);
   302	        const double n = static_cast<double>(N);
   303	        const double gflops = (2.0 * n * n * n) / elapsed / 1e9;
   304	        std::printf("Computation time: %lld ms\n", durationMs);
   305	        std::printf("Performance: %.3f GFLOPS\n", gflops);
   306	    }
   307	
   308	    if (printResults) {
   309	        std::vector<double> result =
   310	            gatherResult(localC, N, localRows, localColumns, rank, worldSize,
   311	                         grid[0], grid[1], MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   312	        if (rank == 0) {
   313	            print_results(result, "MatrixC");
   314	        }
   315	    }
   316	
   317	    int globallyValid = 1;
   318	    if (validate) {
   319	        if (rank == 0) {
   320	            std::printf("Validating result...\n");
   321	        }
   322	        const int locallyValid =
   323	            validateLocalResult(localA, localB, localC, N, localRows,
   324	                                localColumns, rank)
   325	                ? 1
   326	                : 0;
   327	        MPI_Allreduce(&locallyValid, &globallyValid, 1, MPI_INT, MPI_MIN,
   328	                      MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   329	        if (rank == 0) {
   330	            std::printf("Validation: %s\n",
   331	                        globallyValid != 0 ? "PASSED" : "FAILED");
   332	        }
   333	    }
   334	
   335	    MPI_Finalize();
   336	    return globallyValid != 0 ? 0 : 1;
   337	}

+// Divide n elements as evenly as possible, including when parts > n.
+Block getBlock(const size_t n, const int coordinate, const int parts) {

+    const size_t remainder = n % static_cast<size_t>(parts);

+// Each process owns the A rows and B columns needed for its C tile.  The

+// Cache-blocked matrix multiplication for the process-local C tile.  k is
+// ordered exactly as in the scalar implementation for every C element, while
+// j is independent and can be vectorized without a floating-point reduction.
+void matrixMultiply(const double* __restrict__ localA,
+                    const double* __restrict__ localB,
+                    double* __restrict__ localC, const size_t N,
+                    const size_t localRows, const size_t localColumns) {
+    constexpr size_t rowTile = 32;
+    constexpr size_t columnTile = 128;
+    constexpr size_t innerTile = 128;
+
+    for (size_t ii = 0; ii < localRows; ii += rowTile) {
+        const size_t iEnd = std::min(ii + rowTile, localRows);
+        for (size_t jj = 0; jj < localColumns; jj += columnTile) {
+            const size_t jEnd = std::min(jj + columnTile, localColumns);
+            for (size_t kk = 0; kk < N; kk += innerTile) {
+                const size_t kEnd = std::min(kk + innerTile, N);
+                for (size_t k = kk; k < kEnd; ++k) {
+                    const double* __restrict__ bRow =
+                        localB + k * localColumns;
+                    for (size_t i = ii; i < iEnd; ++i) {
+                        const double a = localA[i * N + k];
+                        double* __restrict__ cRow =
+                            localC + i * localColumns;
+#pragma omp simd
+                        for (size_t j = jj; j < jEnd; ++j) {
+                            cRow[j] += a * bRow[j];
+                        }
+                    }
+                }
             }
-            C[i * N + j] = sum;
         }
     }

+// MPI uses int counts in the portable interfaces available on MPI-3 systems.
+// Chunking also lets the benchmark handle rows wider than INT_MAX elements.
+void sendLarge(const double* data, size_t count, const int destination,
+               const int tag, MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Send(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, destination, tag, communicator);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+void receiveLarge(double* data, size_t count, const int source, const int tag,
+                  MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Recv(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, source, tag, communicator,
+                 MPI_STATUS_IGNORE);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+// Assemble C only when the user requests result output.  Rows are received
+// directly into their final locations, so rank zero needs no packed staging
+// buffer and MPI int-count limits do not constrain the matrix size.
+std::vector<double> gatherResult(const std::vector<double>& localC,
+                                 const size_t N, const Block localRows,
+                                 const Block localColumns, const int rank,
+                                 const int worldSize, const int gridRows,
+                                 const int gridColumns,
+                                 MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr int resultTag = 1701;
+
+    if (rank != 0) {
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < localRows.size; ++i) {
+            sendLarge(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                      localColumns.size, 0, resultTag, communicator);
+        }
+        return {};
+    }
+
+    std::vector<double> result(N * N);
+    for (int source = 0; source < worldSize; ++source) {
+        const int rowCoordinate = source / gridColumns;
+        const int columnCoordinate = source % gridColumns;
+        const Block rows = getBlock(N, rowCoordinate, gridRows);
+        const Block columns = getBlock(N, columnCoordinate, gridColumns);
+
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < rows.size; ++i) {
+            double* destination =
+                result.data() + (rows.begin + i) * N + columns.begin;
+            if (source == 0) {
+                std::copy_n(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                            columns.size, destination);
+            } else {
+                receiveLarge(destination, columns.size, source, resultTag,
+                             communicator);
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    return result;
+}
+

-    printf("  -h           Show this help message\n");

+// MPI uses int counts in the portable interfaces available on MPI-3 systems.
+// Chunking also lets the benchmark handle rows wider than INT_MAX elements.
+void sendLarge(const double* data, size_t count, const int destination,
+               const int tag, MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Send(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, destination, tag, communicator);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+void receiveLarge(double* data, size_t count, const int source, const int tag,
+                  MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Recv(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, source, tag, communicator,
+                 MPI_STATUS_IGNORE);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+// Assemble C only when the user requests result output.  Rows are received
+// directly into their final locations, so rank zero needs no packed staging
+// buffer and MPI int-count limits do not constrain the matrix size.
+std::vector<double> gatherResult(const std::vector<double>& localC,
+                                 const size_t N, const Block localRows,
+                                 const Block localColumns, const int rank,
+                                 const int worldSize, const int gridRows,
+                                 const int gridColumns,
+                                 MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr int resultTag = 1701;
+
+    if (rank != 0) {
+        if (localColumns.size != 0) {
+            for (size_t i = 0; i < localRows.size; ++i) {
+                sendLarge(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                          localColumns.size, 0, resultTag, communicator);
+            }
+        }
+        return {};
+    }
+
+    std::vector<double> result(N * N);
+    for (int source = 0; source < worldSize; ++source) {
+        const int rowCoordinate = source / gridColumns;
+        const int columnCoordinate = source % gridColumns;
+        const Block rows = getBlock(N, rowCoordinate, gridRows);
+        const Block columns = getBlock(N, columnCoordinate, gridColumns);
+
+        if (columns.size == 0) {
+            continue;
+        }
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < rows.size; ++i) {
+            double* destination =
+                result.data() + (rows.begin + i) * N + columns.begin;
+            if (source == 0) {
+                std::copy_n(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                            columns.size, destination);
+            } else {
+                receiveLarge(destination, columns.size, source, resultTag,
+                             communicator);
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    return result;
+}

+// Generate pseudo-random values for matrix initialization.
+constexpr double getPseudoRndValue(const size_t N, const size_t i,
+                                   const size_t j) noexcept {
+    return (((i + 1) * (i + j + 1) * 1299709) % (N * N)) /

-void initMatrix(std::vector<double>& mat, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            mat[i * N + j] = getPseudoRndValue(N, i, j);
+struct Block {
+    size_t begin;
+    size_t size;
+};
+
+// Divide n elements as evenly as possible, including when parts > n.
+Block getBlock(const size_t n, const int coordinate, const int parts) {
+    const size_t quotient = n / static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t remainder = n % static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t coord = static_cast<size_t>(coordinate);
+    return {coord * quotient + std::min(coord, remainder),
+            quotient + (coord < remainder ? 1U : 0U)};
+}
+
+// Each process owns the A rows and B columns needed for its C tile.  The
+// deterministic initializer lets every process construct those pieces in
+// place, avoiding distribution collectives and a root-memory bottleneck.
+void initLocalMatrices(std::vector<double>& localA,
+                       std::vector<double>& localB, const size_t N,
+                       const Block rows, const Block columns) {
+    for (size_t localI = 0; localI < rows.size; ++localI) {
+        const size_t globalI = rows.begin + localI;
+        for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+            localA[localI * N + k] = getPseudoRndValue(N, globalI, k);
         }
     }
+
+    // B is stored as N x localColumns.  Thus B and C are contiguous in the
+    // inner j loop of the multiplication kernel.
+    for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+        for (size_t localJ = 0; localJ < columns.size; ++localJ) {
+            const size_t globalJ = columns.begin + localJ;
+            localB[k * columns.size + localJ] =
+                getPseudoRndValue(N, k, globalJ);
+        }
+    }
 }
 
-void matrixMultiply(const std::vector<double>& A, const std::vector<double>& B, 
-                    std::vector<double>& C, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            double sum = 0.0;
-            for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
-                sum += A[i * N + k] * B[k * N + j];
+// Cache-blocked matrix multiplication for the process-local C tile.  k is
+// ordered exactly as in the scalar implementation for every C element, while
+// j is independent and can be vectorized without a floating-point reduction.
+void matrixMultiply(const double* __restrict__ localA,
+                    const double* __restrict__ localB,
+                    double* __restrict__ localC, const size_t N,
+                    const size_t localRows, const size_t localColumns) {
+    constexpr size_t rowTile = 32;
+    constexpr size_t columnTile = 128;
+    constexpr size_t innerTile = 128;
+
+    for (size_t ii = 0; ii < localRows; ii += rowTile) {
+        const size_t iEnd = std::min(ii + rowTile, localRows);
+        for (size_t jj = 0; jj < localColumns; jj += columnTile) {
+            const size_t jEnd = std::min(jj + columnTile, localColumns);
+            for (size_t kk = 0; kk < N; kk += innerTile) {
+                const size_t kEnd = std::min(kk + innerTile, N);
+                for (size_t k = kk; k < kEnd; ++k) {
+                    const double* __restrict__ bRow =
+                        localB + k * localColumns;
+                    for (size_t i = ii; i < iEnd; ++i) {
+                        const double a = localA[i * N + k];
+                        double* __restrict__ cRow =
+                            localC + i * localColumns;
+#pragma omp simd
+                        for (size_t j = jj; j < jEnd; ++j) {
+                            cRow[j] += a * bRow[j];
+                        }
+                    }
+                }
             }
-            C[i * N + j] = sum;
         }
     }
 }

+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());

+// Assemble C only when the user requests result output.  Rows are received

+std::vector<double> gatherResult(const std::vector<double>& localC,
+                                 const size_t N, const Block localRows,
+                                 const Block localColumns, const int rank,
+                                 const int worldSize, const int gridRows,
+                                 const int gridColumns,

+

+// Generate pseudo-random values for matrix initialization.
+constexpr double getPseudoRndValue(const size_t N, const size_t i,
+                                   const size_t j) noexcept {
+    return (((i + 1) * (i + j + 1) * 1299709) % (N * N)) /

-void initMatrix(std::vector<double>& mat, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            mat[i * N + j] = getPseudoRndValue(N, i, j);
+struct Block {
+    size_t begin;
+    size_t size;
+};
+
+// Divide n elements as evenly as possible, including when parts > n.
+Block getBlock(const size_t n, const int coordinate, const int parts) {
+    const size_t quotient = n / static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t remainder = n % static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t coord = static_cast<size_t>(coordinate);
+    return {coord * quotient + std::min(coord, remainder),
+            quotient + (coord < remainder ? 1U : 0U)};
+}
+
+// Each process owns the A rows and B columns needed for its C tile.  The
+// deterministic initializer lets every process construct those pieces in
+// place, avoiding distribution collectives and a root-memory bottleneck.
+void initLocalMatrices(std::vector<double>& localA,
+                       std::vector<double>& localB, const size_t N,
+                       const Block rows, const Block columns) {
+    for (size_t localI = 0; localI < rows.size; ++localI) {
+        const size_t globalI = rows.begin + localI;
+        for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+            localA[localI * N + k] = getPseudoRndValue(N, globalI, k);
         }
     }
+
+    // B is stored as N x localColumns.  Thus B and C are contiguous in the
+    // inner j loop of the multiplication kernel.
+    for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+        for (size_t localJ = 0; localJ < columns.size; ++localJ) {
+            const size_t globalJ = columns.begin + localJ;
+            localB[k * columns.size + localJ] =
+                getPseudoRndValue(N, k, globalJ);
+        }
+    }
 }
 
-void matrixMultiply(const std::vector<double>& A, const std::vector<double>& B, 
-                    std::vector<double>& C, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            double sum = 0.0;
-            for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
-                sum += A[i * N + k] * B[k * N + j];
+// Cache-blocked matrix multiplication for the process-local C tile.  k is
+// ordered exactly as in the scalar implementation for every C element, while
+// j is independent and can be vectorized without a floating-point reduction.
+void matrixMultiply(const double* __restrict__ localA,
+                    const double* __restrict__ localB,
+                    double* __restrict__ localC, const size_t N,
+                    const size_t localRows, const size_t localColumns) {
+    constexpr size_t rowTile = 32;
+    constexpr size_t columnTile = 128;
+    constexpr size_t innerTile = 128;
+
+    for (size_t ii = 0; ii < localRows; ii += rowTile) {
+        const size_t iEnd = std::min(ii + rowTile, localRows);
+        for (size_t jj = 0; jj < localColumns; jj += columnTile) {
+            const size_t jEnd = std::min(jj + columnTile, localColumns);
+            for (size_t kk = 0; kk < N; kk += innerTile) {
+                const size_t kEnd = std::min(kk + innerTile, N);
+                for (size_t k = kk; k < kEnd; ++k) {
+                    const double* __restrict__ bRow =
+                        localB + k * localColumns;
+                    for (size_t i = ii; i < iEnd; ++i) {
+                        const double a = localA[i * N + k];
+                        double* __restrict__ cRow =
+                            localC + i * localColumns;
+#pragma omp simd
+                        for (size_t j = jj; j < jEnd; ++j) {
+                            cRow[j] += a * bRow[j];
+                        }
+                    }
+                }
             }
-            C[i * N + j] = sum;
         }
     }
 }

+// MPI uses int counts in the portable interfaces available on MPI-3 systems.
+// Chunking also lets the benchmark handle rows wider than INT_MAX elements.
+void sendLarge(const double* data, size_t count, const int destination,
+               const int tag, MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Send(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, destination, tag, communicator);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+void receiveLarge(double* data, size_t count, const int source, const int tag,
+                  MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Recv(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, source, tag, communicator,
+                 MPI_STATUS_IGNORE);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+// Assemble C only when the user requests result output.  Rows are received
+// directly into their final locations, so rank zero needs no packed staging
+// buffer and MPI int-count limits do not constrain the matrix size.
+std::vector<double> gatherResult(const std::vector<double>& localC,
+                                 const size_t N, const Block localRows,
+                                 const Block localColumns, const int rank,
+                                 const int worldSize, const int gridRows,
+                                 const int gridColumns,
+                                 MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr int resultTag = 1701;
+
+    if (rank != 0) {
+        if (localColumns.size != 0) {
+            for (size_t i = 0; i < localRows.size; ++i) {
+                sendLarge(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                          localColumns.size, 0, resultTag, communicator);
+            }
+        }
+        return {};
+    }
+
+    std::vector<double> result(N * N);
+    for (int source = 0; source < worldSize; ++source) {
+        const int rowCoordinate = source / gridColumns;
+        const int columnCoordinate = source % gridColumns;
+        const Block rows = getBlock(N, rowCoordinate, gridRows);
+        const Block columns = getBlock(N, columnCoordinate, gridColumns);
+
+        if (columns.size == 0) {
+            continue;
+        }
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < rows.size; ++i) {
+            double* destination =
+                result.data() + (rows.begin + i) * N + columns.begin;
+            if (source == 0) {
+                std::copy_n(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                            columns.size, destination);
+            } else {
+                receiveLarge(destination, columns.size, source, resultTag,
+                             communicator);
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    return result;
+}
+

 }

+// Generate pseudo-random values for matrix initialization.
+constexpr double getPseudoRndValue(const size_t N, const size_t i,
+                                   const size_t j) noexcept {
+    return (((i + 1) * (i + j + 1) * 1299709) % (N * N)) /

-void initMatrix(std::vector<double>& mat, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            mat[i * N + j] = getPseudoRndValue(N, i, j);
+struct Block {
+    size_t begin;
+    size_t size;
+};
+
+// Divide n elements as evenly as possible, including when parts > n.
+Block getBlock(const size_t n, const int coordinate, const int parts) {
+    const size_t quotient = n / static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t remainder = n % static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t coord = static_cast<size_t>(coordinate);
+    return {coord * quotient + std::min(coord, remainder),
+            quotient + (coord < remainder ? 1U : 0U)};
+}
+
+// Each process owns the A rows and B columns needed for its C tile.  The
+// deterministic initializer lets every process construct those pieces in
+// place, avoiding distribution collectives and a root-memory bottleneck.
+void initLocalMatrices(std::vector<double>& localA,
+                       std::vector<double>& localB, const size_t N,
+                       const Block rows, const Block columns) {
+    for (size_t localI = 0; localI < rows.size; ++localI) {
+        const size_t globalI = rows.begin + localI;
+        for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+            localA[localI * N + k] = getPseudoRndValue(N, globalI, k);
         }
     }
+
+    // B is stored as N x localColumns.  Thus B and C are contiguous in the
+    // inner j loop of the multiplication kernel.
+    for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+        for (size_t localJ = 0; localJ < columns.size; ++localJ) {
+            const size_t globalJ = columns.begin + localJ;
+            localB[k * columns.size + localJ] =
+                getPseudoRndValue(N, k, globalJ);
+        }
+    }
 }
 
-void matrixMultiply(const std::vector<double>& A, const std::vector<double>& B, 
-                    std::vector<double>& C, const size_t N) {
-    for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            double sum = 0.0;
-            for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
-                sum += A[i * N + k] * B[k * N + j];
+// Cache-blocked matrix multiplication for the process-local C tile.  k is
+// ordered exactly as in the scalar implementation for every C element, while
+// j is independent and can be vectorized without a floating-point reduction.
+void matrixMultiply(const double* __restrict__ localA,
+                    const double* __restrict__ localB,
+                    double* __restrict__ localC, const size_t N,
+                    const size_t localRows, const size_t localColumns) {
+    constexpr size_t rowTile = 32;
+    constexpr size_t columnTile = 128;
+    constexpr size_t innerTile = 128;
+
+    for (size_t ii = 0; ii < localRows; ii += rowTile) {
+        const size_t iEnd = std::min(ii + rowTile, localRows);
+        for (size_t jj = 0; jj < localColumns; jj += columnTile) {
+            const size_t jEnd = std::min(jj + columnTile, localColumns);
+            for (size_t kk = 0; kk < N; kk += innerTile) {
+                const size_t kEnd = std::min(kk + innerTile, N);
+                for (size_t k = kk; k < kEnd; ++k) {
+                    const double* __restrict__ bRow =
+                        localB + k * localColumns;
+                    for (size_t i = ii; i < iEnd; ++i) {
+                        const double a = localA[i * N + k];
+                        double* __restrict__ cRow =
+                            localC + i * localColumns;
+#pragma omp simd
+                        for (size_t j = jj; j < jEnd; ++j) {
+                            cRow[j] += a * bRow[j];
+                        }
+                    }
+                }
             }
-            C[i * N + j] = sum;
         }
     }
 }

+// MPI uses int counts in the portable interfaces available on MPI-3 systems.
+// Chunking also lets the benchmark handle rows wider than INT_MAX elements.
+void sendLarge(const double* data, size_t count, const int destination,
+               const int tag, MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Send(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, destination, tag, communicator);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+void receiveLarge(double* data, size_t count, const int source, const int tag,
+                  MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr size_t maxCount =
+        static_cast<size_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max());
+    while (count != 0) {
+        const int chunk = static_cast<int>(std::min(count, maxCount));
+        MPI_Recv(data, chunk, MPI_DOUBLE, source, tag, communicator,
+                 MPI_STATUS_IGNORE);
+        data += chunk;
+        count -= static_cast<size_t>(chunk);
+    }
+}
+
+// Assemble C only when the user requests result output.  Rows are received
+// directly into their final locations, so rank zero needs no packed staging
+// buffer and MPI int-count limits do not constrain the matrix size.
+std::vector<double> gatherResult(const std::vector<double>& localC,
+                                 const size_t N, const Block localRows,
+                                 const Block localColumns, const int rank,
+                                 const int worldSize, const int gridRows,
+                                 const int gridColumns,
+                                 MPI_Comm communicator) {
+    constexpr int resultTag = 1701;
+
+    if (rank != 0) {
+        if (localColumns.size != 0) {
+            for (size_t i = 0; i < localRows.size; ++i) {
+                sendLarge(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                          localColumns.size, 0, resultTag, communicator);
+            }
+        }
+        return {};
+    }
+
+    std::vector<double> result(N * N);
+    for (int source = 0; source < worldSize; ++source) {
+        const int rowCoordinate = source / gridColumns;
+        const int columnCoordinate = source % gridColumns;
+        const Block rows = getBlock(N, rowCoordinate, gridRows);
+        const Block columns = getBlock(N, columnCoordinate, gridColumns);
+
+        if (columns.size == 0) {
+            continue;
+        }
+        for (size_t i = 0; i < rows.size; ++i) {
+            double* destination =
+                result.data() + (rows.begin + i) * N + columns.begin;
+            if (source == 0) {
+                std::copy_n(localC.data() + i * localColumns.size,
+                            columns.size, destination);
+            } else {
+                receiveLarge(destination, columns.size, source, resultTag,
+                             communicator);
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    return result;
+}
+

codex
Implemented MPI distributed-memory parallelism while keeping the `matmul` executable name unchanged.

Key changes:

- 2D MPI process-grid decomposition of matrix C.
- Distributed A rows and B columns with balanced uneven partitions.
- Cache-blocked, SIMD-vectorized local multiplication.
- Root-only benchmark output and optional result gathering.
- Distributed validation using `MPI_Allreduce`.
- Timing reports the slowest rank for accurate aggregate GFLOPS.
- CMake now requires and links MPI without adding other runtime dependencies.

Modified files:

- [matmul.cpp](/home/llmtest/evals/20260805-120633/matmul_gpt-5.6-sol-xhigh_mpi_r4/matmul/matmul.cpp:1)
- [CMakeLists.txt](/home/llmtest/evals/20260805-120633/matmul_gpt-5.6-sol-xhigh_mpi_r4/matmul/CMakeLists.txt:1)

Verified clean compilation and correctness with 1, 2, 3, 4, and 8 MPI ranks, including uneven and zero-sized tiles. Results were consistent across rank counts and validation passed. On the test host, the optimized one-rank kernel improved the 512×512 case from approximately 0.38 to 5.2 GFLOPS, with further MPI scaling.

-void initMatrix(std::vector<double>& mat, const size_t N) {

-        for (size_t j = 0; j < N; ++j) {
-            mat[i * N + j] = getPseudoRndValue(N, i, j);
+struct Block {
+    size_t begin;
+    size_t size;
+};
+
+// Divide n elements as evenly as possible, including when parts > n.
+Block getBlock(const size_t n, const int coordinate, const int parts) {
+    const size_t quotient = n / static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t remainder = n % static_cast<size_t>(parts);
+    const size_t coord = static_cast<size_t>(coordinate);
+    return {coord * quotient + std::min(coord, remainder),
+            quotient + (coord < remainder ? 1U : 0U)};
+}
+
+// Each process owns the A rows and B columns needed for its C tile.  The
+// deterministic initializer lets every process construct those pieces in
+// place, avoiding distribution collectives and a root-memory bottleneck.
+void initLocalMatrices(std::vector<double>& localA,
+                       std::vector<double>& localB, const size_t N,
+                       const Block rows, const Block columns) {
+    for (size_t localI = 0; localI < rows.size; ++localI) {
+        const size_t globalI = rows.begin + localI;
+        for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+            localA[localI * N + k] = getPseudoRndValue(N, globalI, k);
         }
     }
+
+    // B is stored as N x localColumns.  Thus B and C are contiguous in the
+    // inner j loop of the multiplication kernel.
+    for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {
+        for (size_t localJ = 0; localJ < columns.size; ++localJ) {
+            const size_t globalJ = columns.begin + localJ;
+            localB[k * columns.size + localJ] =
+                getPseudoRndValue(N, k, globalJ);
+        }
+    }
 }
 
-void matrixMultiply(const std::vector<double>& A, const std::vector<double>& B,
